Memorandum 
          TO:     Mark Rajter – Assistant Superintendent  
                      Business Services   
    FROM:     Kenneth D. Miller – Executive Director  
Facility Operations & 
Purchasing Department 
Troy, Michigan  48083 
     DATE:     28 July 2014 
Phone  248 823-4050 
Fax  248 823-4077  
Invitations were sent to nine potential bidders to with seven responding. After analyzing the 
information provided in the proposals, the lowest qualified bidder was Contract Paper Group,

7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c 
     9th Grade for 2015 – 2016  
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seats will be available for the 2015-2016 school year for grade 
9 at the International Academy East.  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105c resolution fo r an unlimited 
number of 9 th grade students  who attend a district in another county, if that 
intermediate school district is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School 
District boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105c  of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
Schools of Choice program  for th e 2015-2016 school year for an un limited 
number of 9 th grade students who attend a school district in another county if 
that Intermediate Schoo l District is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate 
School District boundaries for the purposes of attending the International 
Academy East program located in the Troy School District.

176
To:  Dr. Richard Machesky, Superintendent  
From:  Mark Washington, Director of Technology  
Date:  July 10, 2014  
Re:  Bond Smart Board Purchases  
After meeting with the Elementary Technology Design Team, a recommendation was made that all 
elementary classrooms serving an average size of 25 students or more should be equipped with a multiple 
touch SmartBoard.  Over the past several years via bond projects, district purchases and PTO 
contributions, SmartBoards have been installed in a majority of the elementary classrooms.   
This recommendation is for $185,012.50 to VSC, Inc for the purchase, installation and configuration of 40 
SmartBoards and 5 Sharp LED Touch TVs for the elementary schools.  This will complete the installation 
of SmartBoards at the elementary schools and match the existing boards to the district standard in all of the 
other elementary classrooms.   
The additional 5 LED TVs will be located on the elementary school stages that are used for instruction.  In 
those places, SmartBoards cannot be mounted on the walls and require a portable unit.  Although Smart 
makes a portable unit with a projector, it requires constant recalibration if the unit is moved or bumped.  
When this frequently happens, it takes away valuable instructional time while recalibrating.  Installation will 
occur this summer. 
These prices are from the REMC Statewide Purchasing Cooperative program.  Attached please find a copy of 
the VSC quote as well as an email from Nancy Corner, REMC Project Director, confirming that pricing is from 
statewide contract and therefore meets individual school district bidding requirements. 
All items recommended for purchase are being expensed from the 2014 Bond Fund.     
Should you have any questions, please feel free to contact me.  
Cc:    J. Keeling 
M. Rajter   
  R. Machesky

vendor can install the new equipment.  The installation component of the bid package for the 
vendor is only to uncrate, set into place and test the equipment.
